========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess... it's just been a real mess lately. Ever since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. She went Army, which honestly surprised me because my dad was Navy. He was on a destroyer in the seventies, and we had this great old picture of him in his dress whites in the hallway. The frame actually broke a couple years ago when I was moving a couch, glass went absolutely everywhere. I swear I was finding little shards in the carpet for months, no matter how many times I vacuumed. 

But, um, anyway... since she left, I just can't keep myself together. I'm literally sitting there crying in my truck before my shifts start. And at work—I'm a sheet metal worker—I'm missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. I usually work with galvanized steel, mostly custom fabrication, and these younger guys on the crew, they just don't know how to handle the materials right. They dull the snips like you wouldn't believe. I've been snapping at them a lot lately. I just have zero patience. 

And when I get home, I don't even go out on the water anymore, or work on my sculpture. I was making this big heron out of scrap aluminum. Herons are cool birds, they just stand so still in the shallows... there's this one spot by the inlet where they always fish at low tide, and you can just watch them for hours. But yeah, I haven't touched the sculpture or the boat. I just don't see the point right now.
=====================================

Well, it's been about a month. Yeah, a month since she left. I remember because we drove her down to the processing station on a Tuesday, and it was just pouring rain. Like, absolute sheets of rain coming down on the highway. My truck usually handles okay in the wet, but the windshield wipers were streaking so bad. I've been meaning to replace those blades since last winter. You know how the rubber just degrades and cracks when it gets cold out? I usually get the heavy-duty silicone ones from the auto parts store on Route 9. The guy there, Jimmy, he always sets a pair aside for me. He’s got a daughter too, actually, she plays softball... they went to state last year, which is pretty cool. 

But, um, anyway, yeah. I've been struggling like this ever since that day she left. Just a solid month of feeling like I can't keep my head above water.

Herons are cool birds, they just stand so still in the shallows... there's this one spot by the inlet where they always fish at low tide, and you can just watch them for hours. You know how the rubber just degrades and cracks when it gets cold out? I usually get the heavy-duty silicone ones from the auto parts store on Route 9. The guy there, Jimmy, he always sets a pair aside for me. He’s got a daughter too, actually, she plays softball... they went to state last year, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah. I've been struggling like this ever since that day she left. Just a solid month of feeling like I can't keep my head above water.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or hopeless almost every day, and has this been going on for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, pretty much every day for the last month. Since she left, it's just this heavy feeling all day long. Like, from the minute I wake up. I usually wake up around 5:30 because the neighbor's dog starts barking. It's this little terrier mix, cute thing, but man, it has a set of lungs on it. They keep it out in the yard, which I don't think is right, especially when it gets chilly. I actually built them a little insulated doghouse out of some scrap diamond plate and foam board I had left over from a job. Diamond plate is great, you know, it doesn't rust if you treat it right, and it looks sharp. We did a whole custom truck bed for this guy out in the county with it once. He was restoring an old F-150, candy apple red... beautiful paint job. But anyway, yeah, I wake up to the dog, and the feeling is just... there. All day. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, tired, yeah. Definitely more tired. It's like my bones are heavy, you know? Walking up the stairs to the apartment feels like I'm wearing steel-toed boots, which I usually do for work, actually. I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. 

But um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.

Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...

Wait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.

He tried to hide it from his mom by wearing high socks for like a year straight, even in the summer. He looked ridiculous. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just don't care about the boat or the sculpture or any of it anymore. It all just feels pointless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, like feeling more tired than usual, blaming yourself for things, or having thoughts that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Well, tired, yeah. Definitely more tired. It's like my bones are heavy, you know? Walking up the stairs to the apartment feels like I'm wearing steel-toed boots, which I usually do for work, actually. I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. \n\nBut um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...\n\nWait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Moving slow? No, I don't think so. I mean, my body feels heavy, like I said, but I'm still moving at my normal pace at work, I think. And restless... not really. I just kind of sit there when I get home. I have this old recliner I bought off Craigslist like five years ago. Best fifty bucks I ever spent. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. He said the chair belonged to his grandfather who used to fall asleep in it watching baseball games. I love baseball, actually. Used to go to the minor league games all the time when I was a kid. My dad would buy me those little plastic helmets they serve the ice cream in. I had a whole collection of them stacked up on my dresser. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. 

But um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. 

But lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. 

Anyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.

I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. 

But um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.

I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. \n\nBut um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. \n\nBut um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in other things that you used to enjoy, even things that were important to you, like watching baseball or working on projects at the shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. \n\nBut lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless every day, and it was noticeably affecting your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a continuous period of at least two weeks where nearly every day you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and you noticed that these feelings clearly interfered with your ability to work, take care of things at home, or enjoy life?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. They dull the snips like you wouldn't believe. I've been snapping at them a lot lately. I just have zero patience. \n\nAnd when I get home, I don't even go out on the water anymore, or work on my sculpture. I was making this big heron out of scrap aluminum. Herons are cool birds, they just stand so still in the shallows... there's this one spot by the inlet where they always fish at low tide, and you can just watch them for hours. But yeah, I haven't touched the sculpture or the boat. I just don't see the point right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? When would you say these feelings really started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month. Yeah, a month since she left. I remember because we drove her down to the processing station on a Tuesday, and it was just pouring rain. Like, absolute sheets of rain coming down on the highway. My truck usually handles okay in the wet, but the windshield wipers were streaking so bad. I've been meaning to replace those blades since last winter. You know how the rubber just degrades and cracks when it gets cold out? I usually get the heavy-duty silicone ones from the auto parts store on Route 9. The guy there, Jimmy, he always sets a pair aside for me. He’s got a daughter too, actually, she plays softball... they went to state last year, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah. I've been struggling like this ever since that day she left. Just a solid month of feeling like I can't keep my head above water.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or hopeless almost every day, and has this been going on for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, pretty much every day for the last month. Since she left, it's just this heavy feeling all day long. Like, from the minute I wake up. I usually wake up around 5:30 because the neighbor's dog starts barking. It's this little terrier mix, cute thing, but man, it has a set of lungs on it. They keep it out in the yard, which I don't think is right, especially when it gets chilly. I actually built them a little insulated doghouse out of some scrap diamond plate and foam board I had left over from a job. Diamond plate is great, you know, it doesn't rust if you treat it right, and it looks sharp. We did a whole custom truck bed for this guy out in the county with it once. He was restoring an old F-150, candy apple red... beautiful paint job. But anyway, yeah, I wake up to the dog, and the feeling is just... there. All day. Just feeling totally down and hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like working on your sculpture or going out on the boat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean, I mentioned the boat and the heron sculpture earlier, right? I just... I haven't even looked at them. The boat's still sitting on the trailer under that blue tarp. Actually, the tarp has a tear in it near the bow. I need to get some of that heavy-duty patch tape, the kind with the reinforced mesh backing. You can get it at the marine supply store down by the harbor. The guy who runs that place, Sal, he's a real character. Always wearing these faded Hawaiian shirts, even in December. He told me once he bought like fifty of them at a swap meet in Florida back in the nineties. Florida's crazy, man. My cousin went down there for spring break once and ended up getting a tattoo of a palm tree on his ankle. He tried to hide it from his mom by wearing high socks for like a year straight, even in the summer. He looked ridiculous. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just don't care about the boat or the sculpture or any of it anymore. It all just feels pointless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, like feeling more tired than usual, blaming yourself for things, or having thoughts that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Well, tired, yeah. Definitely more tired. It's like my bones are heavy, you know? Walking up the stairs to the apartment feels like I'm wearing steel-toed boots, which I usually do for work, actually. I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. \n\nBut um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...\n\nWait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Moving slow? No, I don't think so. I mean, my body feels heavy, like I said, but I'm still moving at my normal pace at work, I think. And restless... not really. I just kind of sit there when I get home. I have this old recliner I bought off Craigslist like five years ago. Best fifty bucks I ever spent. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. He said the chair belonged to his grandfather who used to fall asleep in it watching baseball games. I love baseball, actually. Used to go to the minor league games all the time when I was a kid. My dad would buy me those little plastic helmets they serve the ice cream in. I had a whole collection of them stacked up on my dresser. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. \n\nBut um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in other things that you used to enjoy, even things that were important to you, like watching baseball or working on projects at the shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. \n\nBut lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless every day, and it was noticeably affecting your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a continuous period of at least two weeks where nearly every day you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and you noticed that these feelings clearly interfered with your ability to work, take care of things at home, or enjoy life?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't we just talk about this? Like I was just saying, yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left, and it's definitely messing up my work and everything else. Taking care of things at home? Forget about it. I haven't even taken the trash down to the curb in I don't know how long. The raccoons actually got into it the other night. \n\nI swear, the raccoons in my neighborhood are getting massive. They look like small bears. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she leaves cat food out on her porch for the strays, but I keep telling her she's just feeding the wildlife. She buys that cheap dry food in the massive yellow bags from Costco. Costco is crazy on the weekends, I refuse to go on a Saturday. You spend twenty minutes just looking for a parking spot, and then people block the aisles with their giant carts trying to get the free samples. They had these little pizza bagel samples last time I went, burned the roof of my mouth so bad. The cheese just sticks to you like napalm, you know? You always end up peeling a layer of skin off. \n\nBut yeah, um, I think I already answered this, right? It's been a couple weeks of feeling this way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Loss of energy? I don't really think so. It's not that I'm physically tired or that little things wear me out. I mean, I still get up and make my coffee every morning. I have this stainless steel French press. I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. 

Working with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. You really can't get that smell out of heavy leather, you just have to wait it out. 

But, um... what were you asking? Exhausted? No, I wouldn't say I'm exhausted or out of energy. I just don't care to do the things, you know? The physical energy is there, just... no point to using it.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.2, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... it's just been a real mess lately. Ever since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. She went Army, which honestly surprised me because my dad was Navy. He was on a destroyer in the seventies, and we had this great old picture of him in his dress whites in the hallway. The frame actually broke a couple years ago when I was moving a couch, glass went absolutely everywhere. I swear I was finding little shards in the carpet for months, no matter how many times I vacuumed. \n\nBut, um, anyway... since she left, I just can't keep myself together. I'm literally sitting there crying in my truck before my shifts start. And at work—I'm a sheet metal worker—I'm missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. I usually work with galvanized steel, mostly custom fabrication, and these younger guys on the crew, they just don't know how to handle the materials right. They dull the snips like you wouldn't believe. I've been snapping at them a lot lately. I just have zero patience. \n\nAnd when I get home, I don't even go out on the water anymore, or work on my sculpture. I was making this big heron out of scrap aluminum. Herons are cool birds, they just stand so still in the shallows... there's this one spot by the inlet where they always fish at low tide, and you can just watch them for hours. But yeah, I haven't touched the sculpture or the boat. I just don't see the point right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? When would you say these feelings really started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month. Yeah, a month since she left. I remember because we drove her down to the processing station on a Tuesday, and it was just pouring rain. Like, absolute sheets of rain coming down on the highway. My truck usually handles okay in the wet, but the windshield wipers were streaking so bad. I've been meaning to replace those blades since last winter. You know how the rubber just degrades and cracks when it gets cold out? I usually get the heavy-duty silicone ones from the auto parts store on Route 9. The guy there, Jimmy, he always sets a pair aside for me. He’s got a daughter too, actually, she plays softball... they went to state last year, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah. I've been struggling like this ever since that day she left. Just a solid month of feeling like I can't keep my head above water.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or hopeless almost every day, and has this been going on for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, pretty much every day for the last month. Since she left, it's just this heavy feeling all day long. Like, from the minute I wake up. I usually wake up around 5:30 because the neighbor's dog starts barking. It's this little terrier mix, cute thing, but man, it has a set of lungs on it. They keep it out in the yard, which I don't think is right, especially when it gets chilly. I actually built them a little insulated doghouse out of some scrap diamond plate and foam board I had left over from a job. Diamond plate is great, you know, it doesn't rust if you treat it right, and it looks sharp. We did a whole custom truck bed for this guy out in the county with it once. He was restoring an old F-150, candy apple red... beautiful paint job. But anyway, yeah, I wake up to the dog, and the feeling is just... there. All day. Just feeling totally down and hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like working on your sculpture or going out on the boat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean, I mentioned the boat and the heron sculpture earlier, right? I just... I haven't even looked at them. The boat's still sitting on the trailer under that blue tarp. Actually, the tarp has a tear in it near the bow. I need to get some of that heavy-duty patch tape, the kind with the reinforced mesh backing. You can get it at the marine supply store down by the harbor. The guy who runs that place, Sal, he's a real character. Always wearing these faded Hawaiian shirts, even in December. He told me once he bought like fifty of them at a swap meet in Florida back in the nineties. Florida's crazy, man. My cousin went down there for spring break once and ended up getting a tattoo of a palm tree on his ankle. He tried to hide it from his mom by wearing high socks for like a year straight, even in the summer. He looked ridiculous. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just don't care about the boat or the sculpture or any of it anymore. It all just feels pointless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, like feeling more tired than usual, blaming yourself for things, or having thoughts that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Well, tired, yeah. Definitely more tired. It's like my bones are heavy, you know? Walking up the stairs to the apartment feels like I'm wearing steel-toed boots, which I usually do for work, actually. I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. \n\nBut um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...\n\nWait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Moving slow? No, I don't think so. I mean, my body feels heavy, like I said, but I'm still moving at my normal pace at work, I think. And restless... not really. I just kind of sit there when I get home. I have this old recliner I bought off Craigslist like five years ago. Best fifty bucks I ever spent. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. He said the chair belonged to his grandfather who used to fall asleep in it watching baseball games. I love baseball, actually. Used to go to the minor league games all the time when I was a kid. My dad would buy me those little plastic helmets they serve the ice cream in. I had a whole collection of them stacked up on my dresser. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. \n\nBut um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in other things that you used to enjoy, even things that were important to you, like watching baseball or working on projects at the shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. \n\nBut lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless every day, and it was noticeably affecting your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a continuous period of at least two weeks where nearly every day you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and you noticed that these feelings clearly interfered with your ability to work, take care of things at home, or enjoy life?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't we just talk about this? Like I was just saying, yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left, and it's definitely messing up my work and everything else. Taking care of things at home? Forget about it. I haven't even taken the trash down to the curb in I don't know how long. The raccoons actually got into it the other night. \n\nI swear, the raccoons in my neighborhood are getting massive. They look like small bears. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she leaves cat food out on her porch for the strays, but I keep telling her she's just feeding the wildlife. She buys that cheap dry food in the massive yellow bags from Costco. Costco is crazy on the weekends, I refuse to go on a Saturday. You spend twenty minutes just looking for a parking spot, and then people block the aisles with their giant carts trying to get the free samples. They had these little pizza bagel samples last time I went, burned the roof of my mouth so bad. The cheese just sticks to you like napalm, you know? You always end up peeling a layer of skin off. \n\nBut yeah, um, I think I already answered this, right? It's been a couple weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel exhausting, nearly every day since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Loss of energy? I don't really think so. It's not that I'm physically tired or that little things wear me out. I mean, I still get up and make my coffee every morning. I have this stainless steel French press. I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. \n\nWorking with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. You really can't get that smell out of heavy leather, you just have to wait it out. \n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Exhausted? No, I wouldn't say I'm exhausted or out of energy. I just don't care to do the things, you know? The physical energy is there, just... no point to using it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

No, I don't think so. I still like doing my usual stuff. Like, I still really get a kick out of going down to the auto salvage yard on Sunday mornings. I'm trying to rebuild the carburetor on my '87 F-150, and you just can't find good original parts for those anymore, you have to scavenge. 

The yard I go to is off Route 9, run by this guy named Earl. Earl is a trip. He actually has a glass eye, told me he lost his real one in a deep-sea fishing accident down in the Gulf back in the 70s. Said a marlin whipped its bill back and caught him right in the face. I don't know if I believe him, honestly, he exaggerates everything. Like last week he tried to charge me forty bucks for a rusted-out alternator bracket. Forty bucks! I told him he was out of his mind. You can get a brand new aftermarket one online for twenty-five. The shipping takes forever though, especially with the mail being so messed up lately. My mail carrier keeps putting my neighbor's magazines in my box. They get these weird catalogs for, like, luxury birdhouses. Who spends two hundred dollars on a birdhouse? The squirrels just chew right through the wood anyway to get the seed. We have these huge grey squirrels in my yard, they're practically acrobats... 

But wait, what were we talking about? Losing interest in things? No, I wouldn't say that. I still like my hobbies and everything. I just feel terrible because my daughter's gone, but I still enjoy working on my truck.

They look like small bears. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she leaves cat food out on her porch for the strays, but I keep telling her she's just feeding the wildlife. She buys that cheap dry food in the massive yellow bags from Costco. Costco is crazy on the weekends, I refuse to go on a Saturday. You spend twenty minutes just looking for a parking spot, and then people block the aisles with their giant carts trying to get the free samples. They had these little pizza bagel samples last time I went, burned the roof of my mouth so bad. The cheese just sticks to you like napalm, you know? You always end up peeling a layer of skin off. \n\nBut yeah, um, I think I already answered this, right? It's been a couple weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel exhausting, nearly every day since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Loss of energy? I don't really think so. It's not that I'm physically tired or that little things wear me out. I mean, I still get up and make my coffee every morning. I have this stainless steel French press. I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. \n\nWorking with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. You really can't get that smell out of heavy leather, you just have to wait it out. \n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Exhausted? No, I wouldn't say I'm exhausted or out of energy. I just don't care to do the things, you know? The physical energy is there, just... no point to using it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like nothing really interests you or gives you pleasure anymore, not even things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I still like doing my usual stuff. Like, I still really get a kick out of going down to the auto salvage yard on Sunday mornings. I'm trying to rebuild the carburetor on my '87 F-150, and you just can't find good original parts for those anymore, you have to scavenge. \n\nThe yard I go to is off Route 9, run by this guy named Earl. Earl is a trip. He actually has a glass eye, told me he lost his real one in a deep-sea fishing accident down in the Gulf back in the 70s. Said a marlin whipped its bill back and caught him right in the face. I don't know if I believe him, honestly, he exaggerates everything. Like last week he tried to charge me forty bucks for a rusted-out alternator bracket. Forty bucks! I told him he was out of his mind. You can get a brand new aftermarket one online for twenty-five. The shipping takes forever though, especially with the mail being so messed up lately. My mail carrier keeps putting my neighbor's magazines in my box. They get these weird catalogs for, like, luxury birdhouses. Who spends two hundred dollars on a birdhouse? The squirrels just chew right through the wood anyway to get the seed. We have these huge grey squirrels in my yard, they're practically acrobats... \n\nBut wait, what were we talking about? Losing interest in things? No, I wouldn't say that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, like I was just saying, I still get a lot out of it. It feels good to actually fix something, you know? I was just working on the fuel lines yesterday, actually. I had to use my good flare nut wrenches so I didn't strip the fittings. I bought this beautiful set off the Mac Tools truck a few years back. 

The guy who drives the tool truck, Gary, he always keeps these little bags of homemade beef jerky on his dash for his regulars. He gets them from this local butcher out in the county, Miller's Meats. Miller's makes the absolute best jalapeño cheddar smoked sausages, too. We used to grill them at the shop on Fridays during the summer. My foreman, Dave, he bought this massive charcoal smoker for the shop, but he never cleans the grease trap on the bottom. It actually caught fire once, just this huge plume of thick black smoke right next to the loading dock. We had to use the big industrial fire extinguisher on it, and that yellow chemical powder got all over a fresh pallet of galvanized sheet metal we just had delivered. Galvanized is such a pain to clean, and if you weld it without grinding the coating off first, you get zinc poisoning. Metal fume fever, the old guys call it. Gives you the absolute worst chills, like you have a horrible flu, you just shake and shake in bed all night... 

Um, sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if I still like working on the truck. Yeah, no, the satisfaction is definitely still there. I still enjoy doing my hobbies. It's just... everything else that feels like a total mess since she left. But the truck is fine.

But yeah, I haven't touched the sculpture or the boat. I just don't see the point right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? When would you say these feelings really started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month. Yeah, a month since she left. I remember because we drove her down to the processing station on a Tuesday, and it was just pouring rain. Like, absolute sheets of rain coming down on the highway. My truck usually handles okay in the wet, but the windshield wipers were streaking so bad. I've been meaning to replace those blades since last winter. You know how the rubber just degrades and cracks when it gets cold out? I usually get the heavy-duty silicone ones from the auto parts store on Route 9. The guy there, Jimmy, he always sets a pair aside for me. He’s got a daughter too, actually, she plays softball... they went to state last year, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah. I've been struggling like this ever since that day she left. Just a solid month of feeling like I can't keep my head above water.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or hopeless almost every day, and has this been going on for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, pretty much every day for the last month. Since she left, it's just this heavy feeling all day long. Like, from the minute I wake up. I usually wake up around 5:30 because the neighbor's dog starts barking. It's this little terrier mix, cute thing, but man, it has a set of lungs on it. They keep it out in the yard, which I don't think is right, especially when it gets chilly. I actually built them a little insulated doghouse out of some scrap diamond plate and foam board I had left over from a job. Diamond plate is great, you know, it doesn't rust if you treat it right, and it looks sharp. We did a whole custom truck bed for this guy out in the county with it once. He was restoring an old F-150, candy apple red... beautiful paint job. But anyway, yeah, I wake up to the dog, and the feeling is just... there. All day. Just feeling totally down and hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like working on your sculpture or going out on the boat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean, I mentioned the boat and the heron sculpture earlier, right? I just... I haven't even looked at them. The boat's still sitting on the trailer under that blue tarp. Actually, the tarp has a tear in it near the bow. I need to get some of that heavy-duty patch tape, the kind with the reinforced mesh backing. You can get it at the marine supply store down by the harbor. The guy who runs that place, Sal, he's a real character. Always wearing these faded Hawaiian shirts, even in December. He told me once he bought like fifty of them at a swap meet in Florida back in the nineties. Florida's crazy, man. My cousin went down there for spring break once and ended up getting a tattoo of a palm tree on his ankle. He tried to hide it from his mom by wearing high socks for like a year straight, even in the summer. He looked ridiculous. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, I just don't care about the boat or the sculpture or any of it anymore. It all just feels pointless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, like feeling more tired than usual, blaming yourself for things, or having thoughts that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Well, tired, yeah. Definitely more tired. It's like my bones are heavy, you know? Walking up the stairs to the apartment feels like I'm wearing steel-toed boots, which I usually do for work, actually. I wear the Red Wings, the classic moc-toe ones. They take forever to break in, though. I remember the first week I had my current pair, I had these blisters the size of quarters on my heels. I had to wrap them in duct tape just to get through my shift at the shop. We were doing a massive ductwork job for that new commercial bakery downtown. The smell coming out of that place when they started testing the ovens... unbelievable. Fresh sourdough at like four in the morning. The head baker, this guy named Arturo, he'd bring us out these warm rolls while we were installing the exhaust hoods. He said the secret is in the starter, he's had his going for like twelve years or something crazy. Twelve years just feeding flour and water to a jar on the counter. My mom tried to make sourdough during the pandemic like everyone else, but hers ended up smelling like old gym socks and she threw it out. \n\nBut um, no, I don't really blame myself for her leaving, she had to go, it's the military. And I don't think about... ending it or anything like that. Just really, really tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. It's like I can't keep my head straight at work anymore. I'll be looking at a blueprint, and then my mind just wanders off. Blueprints are tricky anyway, especially the ones we get from this one architectural firm over in the city. They always overcomplicate the HVAC layouts. Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The guy who usually runs the plasma cutter, Dave, he just retired last month. He was there for thirty years, man. We threw him this huge party at the VFW hall, got a keg of that local IPA he likes, the one with the piney taste. I can't stand IPAs, honestly, they taste like you're drinking a Christmas tree. I'm more of a stout girl, you know? Guinness, if I can get it on draft. There's this Irish pub downtown that does a perfect pour, takes like two whole minutes to pour one pint because they let it settle. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...\n\nWait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Moving slow? No, I don't think so. I mean, my body feels heavy, like I said, but I'm still moving at my normal pace at work, I think. And restless... not really. I just kind of sit there when I get home. I have this old recliner I bought off Craigslist like five years ago. Best fifty bucks I ever spent. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. He said the chair belonged to his grandfather who used to fall asleep in it watching baseball games. I love baseball, actually. Used to go to the minor league games all the time when I was a kid. My dad would buy me those little plastic helmets they serve the ice cream in. I had a whole collection of them stacked up on my dresser. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. \n\nBut um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in other things that you used to enjoy, even things that were important to you, like watching baseball or working on projects at the shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. \n\nBut lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless every day, and it was noticeably affecting your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a continuous period of at least two weeks where nearly every day you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and you noticed that these feelings clearly interfered with your ability to work, take care of things at home, or enjoy life?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't we just talk about this? Like I was just saying, yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left, and it's definitely messing up my work and everything else. Taking care of things at home? Forget about it. I haven't even taken the trash down to the curb in I don't know how long. The raccoons actually got into it the other night. \n\nI swear, the raccoons in my neighborhood are getting massive. They look like small bears. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she leaves cat food out on her porch for the strays, but I keep telling her she's just feeding the wildlife. She buys that cheap dry food in the massive yellow bags from Costco. Costco is crazy on the weekends, I refuse to go on a Saturday. You spend twenty minutes just looking for a parking spot, and then people block the aisles with their giant carts trying to get the free samples. They had these little pizza bagel samples last time I went, burned the roof of my mouth so bad. The cheese just sticks to you like napalm, you know? You always end up peeling a layer of skin off. \n\nBut yeah, um, I think I already answered this, right? It's been a couple weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel exhausting, nearly every day since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Loss of energy? I don't really think so. It's not that I'm physically tired or that little things wear me out. I mean, I still get up and make my coffee every morning. I have this stainless steel French press. I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. \n\nWorking with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. You really can't get that smell out of heavy leather, you just have to wait it out. \n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Exhausted? No, I wouldn't say I'm exhausted or out of energy. I just don't care to do the things, you know? The physical energy is there, just... no point to using it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like nothing really interests you or gives you pleasure anymore, not even things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I still like doing my usual stuff. Like, I still really get a kick out of going down to the auto salvage yard on Sunday mornings. I'm trying to rebuild the carburetor on my '87 F-150, and you just can't find good original parts for those anymore, you have to scavenge. \n\nThe yard I go to is off Route 9, run by this guy named Earl. Earl is a trip. He actually has a glass eye, told me he lost his real one in a deep-sea fishing accident down in the Gulf back in the 70s. Said a marlin whipped its bill back and caught him right in the face. I don't know if I believe him, honestly, he exaggerates everything. Like last week he tried to charge me forty bucks for a rusted-out alternator bracket. Forty bucks! I told him he was out of his mind. You can get a brand new aftermarket one online for twenty-five. The shipping takes forever though, especially with the mail being so messed up lately. My mail carrier keeps putting my neighbor's magazines in my box. They get these weird catalogs for, like, luxury birdhouses. Who spends two hundred dollars on a birdhouse? The squirrels just chew right through the wood anyway to get the seed. We have these huge grey squirrels in my yard, they're practically acrobats... \n\nBut wait, what were we talking about? Losing interest in things? No, I wouldn't say that. I still like my hobbies and everything. I just feel terrible because my daughter's gone, but I still enjoy working on my truck.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like working on your truck or going to the salvage yard, don’t bring you the same satisfaction they used to?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I still get a lot out of it. It feels good to actually fix something, you know? I was just working on the fuel lines yesterday, actually. I had to use my good flare nut wrenches so I didn't strip the fittings. I bought this beautiful set off the Mac Tools truck a few years back. \n\nThe guy who drives the tool truck, Gary, he always keeps these little bags of homemade beef jerky on his dash for his regulars. He gets them from this local butcher out in the county, Miller's Meats. Miller's makes the absolute best jalapeño cheddar smoked sausages, too. We used to grill them at the shop on Fridays during the summer. My foreman, Dave, he bought this massive charcoal smoker for the shop, but he never cleans the grease trap on the bottom. It actually caught fire once, just this huge plume of thick black smoke right next to the loading dock. We had to use the big industrial fire extinguisher on it, and that yellow chemical powder got all over a fresh pallet of galvanized sheet metal we just had delivered. Galvanized is such a pain to clean, and if you weld it without grinding the coating off first, you get zinc poisoning. Metal fume fever, the old guys call it. Gives you the absolute worst chills, like you have a horrible flu, you just shake and shake in bed all night... \n\nUm, sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if I still like working on the truck. Yeah, no, the satisfaction is definitely still there. I still enjoy doing my hobbies. It's just... everything else that feels like a total mess since she left. But the truck is fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean, like I just said, I was out there working on the truck yesterday, so that was pretty good. It was actually a really nice afternoon for it. The sun was hitting the driveway just right, you know? My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she was out in her yard too, planting these massive hydrangea bushes. She buys them from this nursery out in the valley, Green Acres or something like that. They have this huge greenhouse setup, just acres and acres of glass roofs. I always wonder how they keep the hail from completely smashing all those panels. We get some wicked hail around here in the spring. A couple years ago, we had hail the size of golf balls, and it totally destroyed the hood of my old Subaru. I had to pull all the dents out myself. I used a stud welder and a slide hammer. Stud welding is actually pretty cool, you just zap these little copper-coated pins right to the bare metal, hook the hammer on, and pop the dent out. Then you just grind off the pin and smooth it out with body filler. Bondo dust gets absolutely everywhere, though. It's like powdered sugar, it just coats every single thing in your garage. You definitely have to wear a good respirator, not just those cheap paper masks from the hardware store. The elastic straps on those cheap ones always snap right when you stretch them over your ears, it drives me crazy... 

Wait, I'm sorry, what did you ask me? A time I felt pleasure? I mean, yeah, I guess just being out there with the truck yesterday was fine. I don't really know what else to tell you.

I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. \n\nWorking with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. I still like doing my usual stuff. Like, I still really get a kick out of going down to the auto salvage yard on Sunday mornings. Like last week he tried to charge me forty bucks for a rusted-out alternator bracket. Forty bucks! I told him he was out of his mind. You can get a brand new aftermarket one online for twenty-five. The shipping takes forever though, especially with the mail being so messed up lately. My mail carrier keeps putting my neighbor's magazines in my box. They get these weird catalogs for, like, luxury birdhouses. Who spends two hundred dollars on a birdhouse? The squirrels just chew right through the wood anyway to get the seed. We have these huge grey squirrels in my yard, they're practically acrobats... \n\nBut wait, what were we talking about? Losing interest in things? No, I wouldn't say that. I still like my hobbies and everything. I bought this beautiful set off the Mac Tools truck a few years back. \n\nThe guy who drives the tool truck, Gary, he always keeps these little bags of homemade beef jerky on his dash for his regulars.
