Gives you the absolute worst chills, like you have a horrible flu, you just shake and shake in bed all night... \n\nUm, sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if I still like working on the truck. Yeah, no, the satisfaction is definitely still there. I still enjoy doing my hobbies. It's just... everything else that feels like a total mess since she left. But the truck is fine.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuine pleasure or interest in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, like I just said, I was out there working on the truck yesterday, so that was pretty good. It was actually a really nice afternoon for it. The sun was hitting the driveway just right, you know? My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she was out in her yard too, planting these massive hydrangea bushes. She buys them from this nursery out in the valley, Green Acres or something like that. They have this huge greenhouse setup, just acres and acres of glass roofs. I always wonder how they keep the hail from completely smashing all those panels. We get some wicked hail around here in the spring. A couple years ago, we had hail the size of golf balls, and it totally destroyed the hood of my old Subaru. I had to pull all the dents out myself. I used a stud welder and a slide hammer. Stud welding is actually pretty cool, you just zap these little copper-coated pins right to the bare metal, hook the hammer on, and pop the dent out. Then you just grind off the pin and smooth it out with body filler. Bondo dust gets absolutely everywhere, though. It's like powdered sugar, it just coats every single thing in your garage. You definitely have to wear a good respirator, not just those cheap paper masks from the hardware store. The elastic straps on those cheap ones always snap right when you stretch them over your ears, it drives me crazy... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what did you ask me? A time I felt pleasure? I mean, yeah, I guess just being out there with the truck yesterday was fine. I don't really know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any moment recently where you felt real pleasure or interest in something, even just for a little while?\n\nPatient: I mean, I literally just told you about yesterday, being out there with the truck. That was a good time. I felt fine doing that. I really don't know what else you want me to say. It's like when Dave, my foreman, asks me if the ductwork is sealed. I tell him yes, I used the mastic on all the joints, and then he asks me again five minutes later if I'm sure it's sealed. Like, yes, Dave, I just told you. He does that all the time. He's so paranoid about failing inspection because last year, the state inspector—this guy with a really terrible comb-over, I mean it started at his ear and went all the way across—he failed us on this massive commercial HVAC job downtown. Said the gauge of the metal was too thin for the pressure rating. We had to tear out like two hundred feet of spiral duct. Spiral duct is actually really neat how they make it, it's just one continuous strip of metal fed through this machine that rolls it and crimps the seam all at once. It's super strong, but if you drop a section, the seam can pop, and then it's basically garbage. We recycle all our scrap, though. The scrap yard pays by the pound, mostly for aluminum and copper. Copper prices are insane right now. People are actually stealing copper wire out of streetlights...\n\nSorry, I'm doing it again. What was the question? A moment of pleasure? Yeah, just yesterday with the truck. I don't know what else to tell you, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall feeling real pleasure or interest in anything, even small things, in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I don't know how many different ways I can say it. Yesterday. Working on the truck. That was it. I don't know what else you're digging for here. It's just like... it's like when you're trying to find a pinhole leak in a radiator, right? You pressurize the system, you spray the soapy water everywhere, and you're just staring, waiting for a bubble. And sometimes there's just no bubble because the leak isn't where you think it is. Maybe the water pump gasket is weeping instead. Those paper gaskets are the worst, honestly. If you don't scrape the mating surface completely clean, perfectly flat, it's going to leak again in a month. You have to use a razor blade, but you have to hold it at a really shallow angle so you don't gouge the aluminum. Aluminum is so soft. We use a lot of aluminum in the shop for custom duct fittings because it doesn't rust, but man, TIG welding it is an absolute nightmare if it's not perfectly clean. You have to use a stainless steel wire brush that's *only* ever been used on aluminum, otherwise you contaminate the weld pool and it just spits and pops at you...\n\nAnyway. Sorry. I'm just... I'm getting frustrated. You keep asking me the exact same thing. Yesterday was fine. I don't have some big list of happy moments for you. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even small things you used to enjoy, like working on your truck, don't feel as satisfying or interesting to you as they used to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... no, not really. I guess working on the truck still feels about the same. It's just something I do. I've always liked turning wrenches, ever since I was a kid helping my dad in the garage. He had this old Ford F-150, two-tone blue and white. Beautiful truck, but the transmission was a total disaster. We pulled that thing out probably three times one summer. Getting the bell housing bolts off the top is a nightmare, you need about three feet of socket extensions and a swivel joint just to reach them from underneath. And you're lying there on your back on a piece of cardboard, getting transmission fluid dripped right in your eye. Transmission fluid smells terrible, by the way. It's got this sharp, chemical smell that just lingers on your skin for days no matter how much Gojo you use. We used to buy Gojo by the gallon jug with the pump on top. The pumice kind, you know? It works great, but if you have a cut on your finger, it burns like crazy. I get cuts on my hands all the time at work, sheet metal is basically just giant razor blades. You have to wear these Kevlar gloves, but they get so hot in the summer, your hands are just sweating buckets inside them...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? The truck? Yeah, no, I haven't noticed it feeling less satisfying or whatever. It's fine. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even things you used to enjoy a lot, like working on your truck, feel less satisfying or interesting to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Are you seriously asking me this again? Like, they want the ductwork to snake around these decorative ceiling beams, which is a nightmare to fabricate. We have to use this special plasma cutter to get the angles right. The bartender there, Liam, he actually is from Ireland, got the accent and everything. He was telling me about how they make the barrels for the whiskey over there...\n\nWait, what was the question? Decisions? Oh, yeah, I can't decide on anything either. Even just figuring out what to eat for dinner takes me like an hour, and then I just give up and don't eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Moving slow? No, I don't think so. I mean, my body feels heavy, like I said, but I'm still moving at my normal pace at work, I think. And restless... not really. I just kind of sit there when I get home. I have this old recliner I bought off Craigslist like five years ago. Best fifty bucks I ever spent. The guy I bought it from lived in this huge Victorian house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch and the stained glass windows. He said the chair belonged to his grandfather who used to fall asleep in it watching baseball games. I love baseball, actually. Used to go to the minor league games all the time when I was a kid. My dad would buy me those little plastic helmets they serve the ice cream in. I had a whole collection of them stacked up on my dresser. I tried to use them to organize my screws and nails in my toolbox once, but they kept tipping over. Tool organization is a whole thing, you know? Some guys at the shop have these crazy expensive Snap-on rolling cabinets, like five grand just for the box. I just use a standard Craftsman, gets the job done. The red paint is chipping off the drawers, though. I was thinking about sanding it down and repainting it matte black, maybe put some new decals on it. \n\nBut um, no, I'm not fidgety or anything. Just heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in other things that you used to enjoy, even things that were important to you, like watching baseball or working on projects at the shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I don't really care about any of it right now. Ever since my daughter left for basic training... it's just, I come home to this dead house. It's so quiet. The silence is actually loud, if that makes sense. Like when you're in a soundproof room. They have those rooms at the university where they test acoustic materials, right? We had to run ductwork into one of those once, and they use these special insulated flex ducts so the air noise doesn't ruin their tests. The fiberglass insulation makes you itch like crazy if you don't wear sleeves. I usually wear these long-sleeve flannel shirts, even in the summer. I get them from this surplus store out on Route 9. The guy who runs it, old military guy, he always has the best stuff. He's got these vintage ammo cans that I use to store my welding rods. Keep the moisture out, you know? Moisture is the enemy of a good weld. You get porosity, and then the whole joint is compromised. \n\nBut lately, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. It's embarrassing. I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop, which isn't like me. And before my shifts, I'm literally sitting in my truck crying. Just... crying. I used to go out on the water on the weekends, take the kayak out, or work on this metal sculpture I was building in the garage. Scrap metal stuff, mostly copper piping and old gears. Copper is getting so expensive, though. People are ripping it out of abandoned houses left and right. There was this whole sting operation on the local news about a recycling yard that was buying stolen copper wire. \n\nAnyway... I just stopped doing all of it. The kayak, the sculpture. I just don't see the point anymore. I can't keep myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless every day, and it was noticeably affecting your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Two weeks? I don't know... maybe? I mean, I definitely feel down. Hopeless, yeah, that's a good word for it. It just feels like there's this heavy wet blanket over everything. Speaking of blankets, I bought this weighted blanket a while back, supposedly it helps with anxiety and sleep. It's filled with these tiny glass beads, like twenty pounds of them. I got the grey one because it matches my sheets. I buy all my bedding at that big box store out by the mall, the one that always smells like cinnamon by the entrance because of that pretzel stand. Man, those pretzels are good. They dip them in butter right after they come out of the oven. I used to get one every time I went, but now I don't even have an appetite for that. The mall is pretty dead these days anyway, half the stores are boarded up. It's sad, really. Used to be the place to go on a Friday night when I was in high school. We'd hang out by the fountain and throw pennies in. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Two weeks? I guess it's been about that long since she left. And yeah, it's definitely affecting my work, like I said about the welding and crying in my truck. I just can't seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a continuous period of at least two weeks where nearly every day you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and you noticed that these feelings clearly interfered with your ability to work, take care of things at home, or enjoy life?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't we just talk about this? Like I was just saying, yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left, and it's definitely messing up my work and everything else. Taking care of things at home? Forget about it. I haven't even taken the trash down to the curb in I don't know how long. The raccoons actually got into it the other night. \n\nI swear, the raccoons in my neighborhood are getting massive. They look like small bears. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she leaves cat food out on her porch for the strays, but I keep telling her she's just feeding the wildlife. She buys that cheap dry food in the massive yellow bags from Costco. Costco is crazy on the weekends, I refuse to go on a Saturday. You spend twenty minutes just looking for a parking spot, and then people block the aisles with their giant carts trying to get the free samples. They had these little pizza bagel samples last time I went, burned the roof of my mouth so bad. The cheese just sticks to you like napalm, you know? You always end up peeling a layer of skin off. \n\nBut yeah, um, I think I already answered this, right? It's been a couple weeks of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel exhausting, nearly every day since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Loss of energy? I don't really think so. It's not that I'm physically tired or that little things wear me out. I mean, I still get up and make my coffee every morning. I have this stainless steel French press. I actually made the housing for it myself at the shop a few years ago. Welded a custom handle out of some scrap 304 stainless. \n\nWorking with stainless is tricky, you gotta use the right shielding gas, usually an argon mix, or you get this ugly sugaring on the back of the weld. It looks like actual burnt sugar, just this crusty black mess. You have to purge the inside of the pipe with argon to prevent it. We go through so many tanks of argon at work. The guy who delivers the gas cylinders, Jimmy, he's been doing that route for like fifteen years. He always brings his golden retriever in the truck with him. Beautiful dog, but she sheds everywhere. I found dog hair on my welding jacket once, and it smelled like burnt hair for a week after a spark hit it. You really can't get that smell out of heavy leather, you just have to wait it out. \n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Exhausted? No, I wouldn't say I'm exhausted or out of energy. I just don't care to do the things, you know? The physical energy is there, just... no point to using it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like nothing really interests you or gives you pleasure anymore, not even things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I still like doing my usual stuff. Like, I still really get a kick out of going down to the auto salvage yard on Sunday mornings. I'm trying to rebuild the carburetor on my '87 F-150, and you just can't find good original parts for those anymore, you have to scavenge. \n\nThe yard I go to is off Route 9, run by this guy named Earl. Earl is a trip. He actually has a glass eye, told me he lost his real one in a deep-sea fishing accident down in the Gulf back in the 70s. Said a marlin whipped its bill back and caught him right in the face. I don't know if I believe him, honestly, he exaggerates everything. Like last week he tried to charge me forty bucks for a rusted-out alternator bracket. Forty bucks! I told him he was out of his mind. You can get a brand new aftermarket one online for twenty-five. The shipping takes forever though, especially with the mail being so messed up lately. My mail carrier keeps putting my neighbor's magazines in my box. They get these weird catalogs for, like, luxury birdhouses. Who spends two hundred dollars on a birdhouse? The squirrels just chew right through the wood anyway to get the seed. We have these huge grey squirrels in my yard, they're practically acrobats... \n\nBut wait, what were we talking about? Losing interest in things? No, I wouldn't say that. I still like my hobbies and everything. I just feel terrible because my daughter's gone, but I still enjoy working on my truck.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like working on your truck or going to the salvage yard, don’t bring you the same satisfaction they used to?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I still get a lot out of it. It feels good to actually fix something, you know? I was just working on the fuel lines yesterday, actually. I had to use my good flare nut wrenches so I didn't strip the fittings. I bought this beautiful set off the Mac Tools truck a few years back. \n\nThe guy who drives the tool truck, Gary, he always keeps these little bags of homemade beef jerky on his dash for his regulars. He gets them from this local butcher out in the county, Miller's Meats. Miller's makes the absolute best jalapeño cheddar smoked sausages, too. We used to grill them at the shop on Fridays during the summer. My foreman, Dave, he bought this massive charcoal smoker for the shop, but he never cleans the grease trap on the bottom. It actually caught fire once, just this huge plume of thick black smoke right next to the loading dock. We had to use the big industrial fire extinguisher on it, and that yellow chemical powder got all over a fresh pallet of galvanized sheet metal we just had delivered. Galvanized is such a pain to clean, and if you weld it without grinding the coating off first, you get zinc poisoning. Metal fume fever, the old guys call it. Gives you the absolute worst chills, like you have a horrible flu, you just shake and shake in bed all night... \n\nUm, sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if I still like working on the truck. Yeah, no, the satisfaction is definitely still there. I still enjoy doing my hobbies. It's just... everything else that feels like a total mess since she left. But the truck is fine.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuine pleasure or interest in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, like I just said, I was out there working on the truck yesterday, so that was pretty good. It was actually a really nice afternoon for it. The sun was hitting the driveway just right, you know? My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she was out in her yard too, planting these massive hydrangea bushes. She buys them from this nursery out in the valley, Green Acres or something like that. They have this huge greenhouse setup, just acres and acres of glass roofs. I always wonder how they keep the hail from completely smashing all those panels. We get some wicked hail around here in the spring. A couple years ago, we had hail the size of golf balls, and it totally destroyed the hood of my old Subaru. I had to pull all the dents out myself. I used a stud welder and a slide hammer. Stud welding is actually pretty cool, you just zap these little copper-coated pins right to the bare metal, hook the hammer on, and pop the dent out. Then you just grind off the pin and smooth it out with body filler. Bondo dust gets absolutely everywhere, though. It's like powdered sugar, it just coats every single thing in your garage. You definitely have to wear a good respirator, not just those cheap paper masks from the hardware store. The elastic straps on those cheap ones always snap right when you stretch them over your ears, it drives me crazy... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what did you ask me? A time I felt pleasure? I mean, yeah, I guess just being out there with the truck yesterday was fine. I don't really know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any moment recently where you felt real pleasure or interest in something, even just for a little while?\n\nPatient: I mean, I literally just told you about yesterday, being out there with the truck. That was a good time. I felt fine doing that. I really don't know what else you want me to say. It's like when Dave, my foreman, asks me if the ductwork is sealed. I tell him yes, I used the mastic on all the joints, and then he asks me again five minutes later if I'm sure it's sealed. Like, yes, Dave, I just told you. He does that all the time. He's so paranoid about failing inspection because last year, the state inspector—this guy with a really terrible comb-over, I mean it started at his ear and went all the way across—he failed us on this massive commercial HVAC job downtown. Said the gauge of the metal was too thin for the pressure rating. We had to tear out like two hundred feet of spiral duct. Spiral duct is actually really neat how they make it, it's just one continuous strip of metal fed through this machine that rolls it and crimps the seam all at once. It's super strong, but if you drop a section, the seam can pop, and then it's basically garbage. We recycle all our scrap, though. The scrap yard pays by the pound, mostly for aluminum and copper. Copper prices are insane right now. People are actually stealing copper wire out of streetlights...\n\nSorry, I'm doing it again. What was the question? A moment of pleasure? Yeah, just yesterday with the truck. I don't know what else to tell you, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall feeling real pleasure or interest in anything, even small things, in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I don't know how many different ways I can say it. Yesterday. Working on the truck. That was it. I don't know what else you're digging for here. It's just like... it's like when you're trying to find a pinhole leak in a radiator, right? You pressurize the system, you spray the soapy water everywhere, and you're just staring, waiting for a bubble. And sometimes there's just no bubble because the leak isn't where you think it is. Maybe the water pump gasket is weeping instead. Those paper gaskets are the worst, honestly. If you don't scrape the mating surface completely clean, perfectly flat, it's going to leak again in a month. You have to use a razor blade, but you have to hold it at a really shallow angle so you don't gouge the aluminum. Aluminum is so soft. We use a lot of aluminum in the shop for custom duct fittings because it doesn't rust, but man, TIG welding it is an absolute nightmare if it's not perfectly clean. You have to use a stainless steel wire brush that's *only* ever been used on aluminum, otherwise you contaminate the weld pool and it just spits and pops at you...\n\nAnyway. Sorry. I'm just... I'm getting frustrated. You keep asking me the exact same thing. Yesterday was fine. I don't have some big list of happy moments for you. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even small things you used to enjoy, like working on your truck, don't feel as satisfying or interesting to you as they used to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... no, not really. I guess working on the truck still feels about the same. It's just something I do. I've always liked turning wrenches, ever since I was a kid helping my dad in the garage. He had this old Ford F-150, two-tone blue and white. Beautiful truck, but the transmission was a total disaster. We pulled that thing out probably three times one summer. Getting the bell housing bolts off the top is a nightmare, you need about three feet of socket extensions and a swivel joint just to reach them from underneath. And you're lying there on your back on a piece of cardboard, getting transmission fluid dripped right in your eye. Transmission fluid smells terrible, by the way. It's got this sharp, chemical smell that just lingers on your skin for days no matter how much Gojo you use. We used to buy Gojo by the gallon jug with the pump on top. The pumice kind, you know? It works great, but if you have a cut on your finger, it burns like crazy. I get cuts on my hands all the time at work, sheet metal is basically just giant razor blades. You have to wear these Kevlar gloves, but they get so hot in the summer, your hands are just sweating buckets inside them...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? The truck? Yeah, no, I haven't noticed it feeling less satisfying or whatever. It's fine. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even things you used to enjoy a lot, like working on your truck, feel less satisfying or interesting to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Are you seriously asking me this again? I... I just answered this. Like, literally two seconds ago. No, it doesn't feel less satisfying. It's the same. I still like it. I don't know why you're trying to make me say I don't. It's like you're trying to force a square peg into a round hole, you know? Like when guys try to use a half-inch drill bit to widen a hole that's only supposed to be three-eighths, and they end up just tearing the metal and making this jagged, awful mess. And then they try to cover it up with a washer, but the washer doesn't sit flat because the burrs are still there. You always have to deburr your holes. We have this little tool, it looks like a pen but it has a curved, spinning blade on the end. You just run it around the edge of the hole and it takes the sharp edge right off. It's so satisfying to use, actually. Makes these little curly shavings. But they get everywhere. They get in your boots, and then you're walking around all day feeling like you have a tiny needle stabbing the bottom of your foot. I wear steel-toe Red Wings, they're heavy as hell but they last forever if you oil them. You have to use mink oil, not that cheap synthetic stuff... \n\nLook, I'm doing it again. But seriously, why do you keep asking me the exact same question? I haven't lost interest in the truck. It's fine.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even things you used to enjoy a lot, like working on your truck, feel less satisfying or interesting to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Okay, is this a joke? Are you reading off a broken screen or something? I literally just told you. No. The truck is fine. I still enjoy it. It hasn't changed. \n\nI feel like I'm talking to our CNC plasma cutter when the G-code gets corrupted. We have this massive 5-by-10 foot water table at the shop for cutting flat patterns, right? And sometimes the software just glitches, and the torch head will just sit there and pierce the exact same spot over and over and over again until it blows a massive, ugly crater through the sheet metal. And you're just standing there slamming the big red emergency stop button, but it doesn't even register. The E-stop button on our machine is all sticky anyway because guys touch it with gloves covered in cutting fluid. That fluid gets absolutely rancid if you don't aerate the water tank. It smells like rotten eggs and old pennies, I swear. You're supposed to put this specific biocide in it to kill the bacteria, but my boss is so cheap he buys this knock-off brand, and it just makes the water foam up like a rabid dog... \n\nAnyway. I'm sorry, I'm just... my head is spinning. Why do you keep asking me about the truck? I haven't lost interest in it. Please ask me something else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess... yeah. Other things. Since my daughter left for basic training, I just... I stopped going out on the water, and I haven't touched my metal sculptures at all. I just don't see the point right now. 

I make these sculptures out of scrap from the shop, you know? Mostly stainless steel. Sometimes aluminum, but aluminum is a total pain to TIG weld. You have to use AC current to blast off the oxide layer, and if your tungsten gets even a little bit contaminated, you have to stop and grind it down. The bench grinder we have at the shop has a wheel that's completely unbalanced. It vibrates so hard it literally walks across the table if it's not bolted down. One of the younger guys, Jason, he tried to use it with gloves on the other day. You never wear gloves near a rotating wheel! It'll catch the fabric and pull your whole hand right in. I snapped at him pretty bad. 

Actually, I've been snapping at all the younger guys lately. I just can't keep myself together. I'm making stupid mistakes, too. Missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. It's embarrassing. But I just... I'm sitting in my truck crying before my shifts even start. And then I come home to this completely dead house. 

She took all her posters down before she left, you know? Wanted a clean slate for the Army. Left these little blue sticky tack marks all over her bedroom walls. I tried scrubbing them off with a Magic Eraser, but those things just take the paint right off if you rub too hard. They're basically just micro-sandpaper. Melamine foam, I think it's called. They use it for soundproofing in recording studios, too, which is weird to think about. A whole room covered in cleaning sponges... 

But yeah. The sculptures, the water... I don't care about any of it right now. I just feel like I'm falling apart.

You pressurize the system, you spray the soapy water everywhere, and you're just staring, waiting for a bubble. And sometimes there's just no bubble because the leak isn't where you think it is. Maybe the water pump gasket is weeping instead. Those paper gaskets are the worst, honestly. If you don't scrape the mating surface completely clean, perfectly flat, it's going to leak again in a month. You have to use a razor blade, but you have to hold it at a really shallow angle so you don't gouge the aluminum. Aluminum is so soft. We use a lot of aluminum in the shop for custom duct fittings because it doesn't rust, but man, TIG welding it is an absolute nightmare if it's not perfectly clean. You have to use a stainless steel wire brush that's *only* ever been used on aluminum, otherwise you contaminate the weld pool and it just spits and pops at you...\n\nAnyway. Sorry. I'm just... I'm getting frustrated. You keep asking me the exact same thing. Yesterday was fine. I don't have some big list of happy moments for you. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even small things you used to enjoy, like working on your truck, don't feel as satisfying or interesting to you as they used to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... no, not really. I guess working on the truck still feels about the same. It's just something I do. I've always liked turning wrenches, ever since I was a kid helping my dad in the garage. He had this old Ford F-150, two-tone blue and white. Beautiful truck, but the transmission was a total disaster. We pulled that thing out probably three times one summer. Getting the bell housing bolts off the top is a nightmare, you need about three feet of socket extensions and a swivel joint just to reach them from underneath. And you're lying there on your back on a piece of cardboard, getting transmission fluid dripped right in your eye. Transmission fluid smells terrible, by the way. It's got this sharp, chemical smell that just lingers on your skin for days no matter how much Gojo you use. We used to buy Gojo by the gallon jug with the pump on top. The pumice kind, you know? It works great, but if you have a cut on your finger, it burns like crazy. I get cuts on my hands all the time at work, sheet metal is basically just giant razor blades. You have to wear these Kevlar gloves, but they get so hot in the summer, your hands are just sweating buckets inside them...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? The truck? Yeah, no, I haven't noticed it feeling less satisfying or whatever. It's fine. It's just the truck.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even things you used to enjoy a lot, like working on your truck, feel less satisfying or interesting to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Are you seriously asking me this again? I... I just answered this. Like, literally two seconds ago. No, it doesn't feel less satisfying. It's the same. I still like it. I don't know why you're trying to make me say I don't. It's like you're trying to force a square peg into a round hole, you know? Like when guys try to use a half-inch drill bit to widen a hole that's only supposed to be three-eighths, and they end up just tearing the metal and making this jagged, awful mess. And then they try to cover it up with a washer, but the washer doesn't sit flat because the burrs are still there. You always have to deburr your holes. We have this little tool, it looks like a pen but it has a curved, spinning blade on the end. You just run it around the edge of the hole and it takes the sharp edge right off. It's so satisfying to use, actually. Makes these little curly shavings. But they get everywhere. They get in your boots, and then you're walking around all day feeling like you have a tiny needle stabbing the bottom of your foot. I wear steel-toe Red Wings, they're heavy as hell but they last forever if you oil them. You have to use mink oil, not that cheap synthetic stuff... \n\nLook, I'm doing it again. But seriously, why do you keep asking me the exact same question? I haven't lost interest in the truck. It's fine.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that even things you used to enjoy a lot, like working on your truck, feel less satisfying or interesting to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Okay, is this a joke? Are you reading off a broken screen or something? I literally just told you. No. The truck is fine. I still enjoy it. It hasn't changed. \n\nI feel like I'm talking to our CNC plasma cutter when the G-code gets corrupted. We have this massive 5-by-10 foot water table at the shop for cutting flat patterns, right? And sometimes the software just glitches, and the torch head will just sit there and pierce the exact same spot over and over and over again until it blows a massive, ugly crater through the sheet metal. And you're just standing there slamming the big red emergency stop button, but it doesn't even register. The E-stop button on our machine is all sticky anyway because guys touch it with gloves covered in cutting fluid. That fluid gets absolutely rancid if you don't aerate the water tank. It smells like rotten eggs and old pennies, I swear. You're supposed to put this specific biocide in it to kill the bacteria, but my boss is so cheap he buys this knock-off brand, and it just makes the water foam up like a rabid dog... \n\nAnyway. I'm sorry, I'm just... my head is spinning. Why do you keep asking me about the truck? I haven't lost interest in it. Please ask me something else.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed that things other than working on your truck don’t feel as enjoyable or satisfying as they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess... yeah. Other things. Since my daughter left for basic training, I just... I stopped going out on the water, and I haven't touched my metal sculptures at all. I just don't see the point right now. \n\nI make these sculptures out of scrap from the shop, you know? Mostly stainless steel. Sometimes aluminum, but aluminum is a total pain to TIG weld. You have to use AC current to blast off the oxide layer, and if your tungsten gets even a little bit contaminated, you have to stop and grind it down. The bench grinder we have at the shop has a wheel that's completely unbalanced. It vibrates so hard it literally walks across the table if it's not bolted down. One of the younger guys, Jason, he tried to use it with gloves on the other day. You never wear gloves near a rotating wheel! It'll catch the fabric and pull your whole hand right in. I snapped at him pretty bad. \n\nActually, I've been snapping at all the younger guys lately. I just can't keep myself together. I'm making stupid mistakes, too. Missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. It's embarrassing. But I just... I'm sitting in my truck crying before my shifts even start. And then I come home to this completely dead house. \n\nShe took all her posters down before she left, you know? Wanted a clean slate for the Army. Left these little blue sticky tack marks all over her bedroom walls. I tried scrubbing them off with a Magic Eraser, but those things just take the paint right off if you rub too hard. They're basically just micro-sandpaper. Melamine foam, I think it's called. They use it for soundproofing in recording studios, too, which is weird to think about. A whole room covered in cleaning sponges... \n\nBut yeah. The sculptures, the water... I don't care about any of it right now. I just feel like I'm falling apart."
